On the morning of the 25th of Marchthe prisoner came to my father's, at the Ship public-house, in Shakspeare-walk; there was a seafaring man there whom he seemed to have some knowledge of, and he said to him,"Ever since I saw you yesterday morning, I have been upon the spree - I have spent 2l. and parted with my watch, and all I have is this," (holding up this George the Second sixpence, tied to the ribbon,) and he asked the man what it was, the man said, "I don't know;" I said, "Let me look at it" - I said, "Any one might know that;" the prisoner had some gin and had no money to pay for it; my father said he ought to be ashamed - he said, "Here, d - n you, take this, it is worth it, I will call and have it again" - the officer came, and I gave it to him.
JOHN CRAWLEY. I am a Police-constable. The prosecutor came to me on the 25th of March, and gave me information - I went to the Three Crowns, where the prisoner was; he denied knowing any thing of the watch or money - I took him outside and said I would take him to the watch-house; he said he would take me to the house which he was at the night before - I went to the White Lion, in the same street, not many doors off; the landlord said he knew him, and he had offered a watch in pawn for 5s., which he refused to let him have.
Q. Had you mentioned to him that the watch had been lost? A. Yes, it has not been found since.
(Ribbon and sixpence produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. That ribbon was not on the watch; he laid it down on the table and said, "Take care of that, I am drunk;" I said, "I am not much better;" he then told me to take care of his watch - I said, "You have a watch-pocket, and I have not," but he gave it to me - I put it into my pocket, and lost it - we were both drunk.
GUILTY. Aged 43. - Judgement Respited .
[trialAccount t18310407-198]
893. JOSEPH SMITH , alias THOMAS , was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of March, 1 gown, value 6s., the goods of Thomas Carman.
SARAH CARMAN. I am the wife of Thomas Carman, a Police-constable. On the 19th of March, about twelve o'clock, I saw the prisoner - he was a stranger; when I came down stairs he was standing in our passage - I had hung a gown out in the yard about half-past nine o'clock that morning, and it was then gone.
ELIZABETH BALEY. I am a dress-maker, and live opposite the prosecutor's, which is the Police-station. On the 19th of March I was sitting at work, and my mother was cleaning the window - she said, "There is a boy there, a very suspicious character; he went into the station-house empty handed, and came out with something in his apron" - we looked, and saw the prisoner with a gown hanging out behind him; I ran down, and sent a young man after him - he was brought back; Mrs. Ackroid came into her passage, and we took it from him; I kept him till the Police-officer came down and took him.
ANN ACKROID. On the 19th of March Baley told me what she had seen - the prisoner was brought back, and we took the gown from him.
WILLIAM SHEEN. I am a Police-constable. I took the prisoner, and have the gown.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was passing, and saw a man chuck the gown on the stairs; he said, "Pick it up;" I took it up, and put it into my apron.
GUILTY. Aged 15. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-199]
894. THOMAS BROWN & WILLIAM WARDELL were indicted for stealing, on the 17th of March, 2 brushes, value 1s. 6d., the goods of James Thomason.
GEORGE KEMP. I am a Police-constable. On the 17th of March I saw the prisoners at the prosecutor's house - I watched them; they passed the shop two or three times, and at last they came away with two brushes in their possession - I cannot say where they took them from, but they had been about the house some time; I took them about fifty yards off - I found these brushes on Brown, but they had both been together, and I saw them examining the brushes.
JAMES THOMASON. I am a cabinet-maker, and live in St. Luke's. I keep this shop; these brushes were tied up, and hanging on a nail in a piece of ground opposite my house - they had been cut off; I saw them safe at seven o'clock, and before eight they were brought back.
Brown's Defence. I picked up these brushes - I over took this boy, and was showing them to him - the Policeman then came.
Wardell's Defence. I was coming along, and this young man said he had found some brushes - he pulled one out of his pocket, and was showing it to me when the Policeman took us.
JURY to GEORGE KEMP. Q. Had you seen them together? A. Yes, and had watched them about from place to place for three-quarters of an hour.
BROWN - GUILTY. Aged 18.
WARDELL - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Confined One Month and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18310407-200]
895. JAMES BLACKFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of February, 2 hats, value 30s., the goods of James Hodges.
WILLIAM MATTHEWS. I am a Police-constable. On the 21st of October, about half-past three o'clock, I saw the prisoner and another lad near the prosecutor's window, in Bethnal-green-road; the prisoner went in, and came out with two hats - I pursued him, and he dropped them; I took them up, and then took him.
JAMES HODGES. I keep an earthenware-shop in Bethnal-green-road. These are my hats - I know nothing of the prisoner.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was walking down the road - I heard the Policeman cry Stop thief! I turned, and he took me to the shop; the people said they had lost two hats, but they did not say I took them from there - he then took me to the watch-house, and as we were going he asked if I had any money; he said if I would give him a few shillings I should not be hurt, he would make it all right.
WILLIAM MATTHEWS. I never lost sight of him; I saw him drop the hats close to his heels - I stopped to pick them up, and he gained ground on me.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18310407-201]
896. JOHN BARNES was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of March, 1 watch, value 30s., the goods of Joseph Matters.
JOSEPH MATTERS. I live in Crown-street, Soho. I have known the prisoner two months or rather more; he worked for me, and lodged in my house - I am a straw and leghorn hat manufacturer; I lost my watch from my kitchen on the 22nd of March - the prisoner had left me, and said he had got a ship the day before, but he slept there that night, and I missed the watch in the morning - his sister is a respectable young woman, and has worked for me many years.
ROBERT TUBLE. I am an officer. I heard of this case, and took the prisoner in the Minories on the 22nd of March, I think; I found this watch on him - he said he bought it at Malta.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18310407-202]
897. CHARLES BARTLETT was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of March, 1 sovereign, the money of John Pagelson, his master.
SUSANNAH PAGELSON. I am the the wife of John Pagelson, a pastrycook and confectioner, who lives in Whitechapel-road. The prisoner has been in our service for two years; he had 1s. a week - he was with me on the 16th of March - at eleven o'clock that night I received a sovereign and locked it in my husband's desk; I went to bed, and all my servants were gone to bed - the prisoner went into the bakehouse, where he sleeps; he was an errand-boy - a little boy, who is here, was taken ill in the night; he went down, and saw a light in the shop - he looked in, and saw the prisoner; I got up at half-past six o'clock - the prisoner was then gone, and the desk had been opened, I suppose by a false-key, and the sovereign was gone - the prisoner had given me no notice of leaving.
WILLIAM SACKETT. I am errand-boy to the prosecutor. On the 17th of March, between one and two o'clock in the morning, I had to come down stairs - I saw a light in the shop, which rather alarmed me, but I looked through the kitchen window, and saw it was the prisoner; he had a candle close to my master's desk - I wondered how he got there, as the doors were locked; I tried them, and they were fast - he must have got up through a trap-door behind the counter; I went to tell my mistress, and when I came back he was gone, and the light also.
SAMUEL PRENDERGRASS. I am an officer. I took the prisoner in his master's house on the 17th of March - I searched him, and found 3s. 4d. in his jacket pocket; I then asked him if he had any more money - he said No, but on further search I found three half-crowns and three shillings in his coat pocket; I questioned him further, and he said he had been out for two hours.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in the habit of turning off the gas, and that night I smelt it very strong - I pulled up the flap, and went down into the cellar; I found a vacancy- I went to try the burners; they were all right, and I went to bed again - I went out in the morning, and when I returned I was taken.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-203]
898. THOMAS BURTON was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of March, 1 purse, value 4s.; 4 half-crowns, 5 shillings, 3 sixpences, and 6d. in copper monies, the property of Sarah Elizabeth Bottley.
SARAH ELIZABETH BOTTLEY. I live in Swan-place, Plummer-street, City-road - I see gentlemen. On the 27th of March, about half-past ten o'clock, I was standing at my door; the prisoner came by - he was a perfect stranger; he addressed me first, and asked if he might be allowed to walk in - I said he might if he liked, but I had an old lady, her daughter, and son there; he said he did not mind, we would have something to drink altogether, and he would send for something - he came in, and sent my servant with 1s. 6d. for some brandy and gin, which was brought; he and the old lady drank the brandy, and we drank the gin - while my servant was gone he said he would give me four half-crowns to be with me that night, which I accepted; he gave me the four half-crowns, and I put them into my purse - he then sent another 1s. 6d. for more brandy and gin, and he gave 1s. for some cheese; my purse had 2s. 6 1/2d. in it before I put the four half-crowns in - the halfpenny had five holes in it; I had some loose silver and copper in my pocket, but could not say how much - we went to bed about half-past twelve o'clock; I took off my pocket, which had the purse in it, wrapped it in my stays, and put them on the drawers - I was awoke about three or four o'clock by my servant, who said she did not think all was right; I then saw the prisoner dressed, and standing near the drawers - he had been undressed when he went to bed; he heard my servant say she did not think all was right, as she had heard him about the room for half an hour - she said I had better get up and see that all was right; I got up, looked into my pocket, and said I had not got a farthing - I accused him of having my money; he said, "No, I have not;" my servant went for an officer by my desire - he was searched in my presence, and my purse was taken from him - he had denied having it, and said most likely my servant had it; when the Policeman came he again denied having it, but it was found inside the lining of his trousers, near the calf of his leg - the Policeman also found about him two half-crowns, two shillings, a sixpence, and 2 1/2d.; I can swear to the purse, and the halfpenny with five holes was in it.
Prisoner. Q.Were you perfectly sober? A. Yes, with the exception of the share of the liquor I had at your expence, which was very little - some of the gin was left in the bottle; I had my perfect recollection - there were six chairs in my sitting-room.
SARAH ADAMS. I am servant to the prosecutrix - when I first lived with her she was under the care of a gentleman who has gone to sea. On the night stated I was sent out by the prisoner, who was with my mistress, for brandy and gin; I had never seen him there before - he and the elderly lady drank the brandy; her daughter, son, my mistress, and I drank the gin - the prisoner took none of that; I went out a second time for 1s. 6d. worth more liquor, which was disposed of in the same way - I then fetched - lb. of cheese, for which the prisoner gave me 1s. and I paid 7 1/2d. for it; the prisoner went to bed with my mistress - he awoke me by walking about the room, but I cannot say the time; I slept in the adjoining room - I called, and asked if he wanted to go out; he said No - I laid down again; I heard him rustling about again forabout half an hour - I again asked if he wanted to go out; he said Yes - I said, "I will get you a light;" I got on my gown and shawl, and said,"I will let you down," but I first awoke my mistress, and asked if all was right - I then went down for a light; when I came up my mistress was standing with her pocket in her hand, empty, and she said, "Adams, I have not a farthing in the world;" I said, "Perhaps you have put your money somewhere;" she said, "No, I am certain I put it into my pocket, and rolled the pocket up in my stays;" I looked about every where I thought it could be - she then accused the prisoner, and said, "You must have my purse and money;" she said, "Give me the purse, and I will forgive you all, for my friend gave it me, and I would not lose it for the world;" he denied it, and said he had not got it, she might search him, and she did put her hand inside his pockets-I said to him, "You must have it;" he said, "It is more likely you have got it a great deal;" my mistress then cried, and said, "Do give it me, there is a good man;" he still denied having it - she then said, "I will not lose my purse," and she desired me to get an officer; when the officer came the prisoner still denied it, but the officer found the purse within the lining of his trousers, near the calf of his leg - he had denied over and over again having the purse or money.
Prisoner. Q. Can you tell the number of persons who were sitting in the room? A. Yes, there were five besides myself - I did not state at Worship-street that there were seven; I did not say, "Have you fleeced him?" she is not a person of that character.
JAMES CLAY. I am a Police-constable. I was called to that house between three and four o'clock on the Sunday morning; I went up stairs, and found the prisoner in the bed-room - he was dressed; the servant had come for me; the prosecutrix was partly dressed - she accused the prisoner of stealing a purse, containing 12s. 6 1/2d.; he denied any knowledge of it till I found it in the lining of his trousers, just below the knee - this is the purse; there is 12s. 6 1/2d. in it - the halfpenny has five holes in it.
Prisoner. Q. Did I deny having any money in my pocket independent of that purse? A. No - I found on you two half-crowns, two shillings, a sixpence, and 2 1/2d.; it was taken from you at the station, but the inspector returned it to you next morning.
S.E. BOTTLEY. This is my purse, and the money in it; here is the halfpenny with five holes in it.
Prisoner. You will find in the deposition that she stated she had four shillings loose in her pocket, and there were not four shillings found - her servant said to her,"Mrs. Jones, is all right, have you fleeced him?" the truth is, she was in such a disgusting state of intoxication that I got up, and said I would not stay with her - when she got to the watch-house, and found I had more money about me, she wanted to make a further charge; I am a clerk in the City.
JAMES CLAY. The prosecutrix was perfectly sober.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-204]
899. GEORGE CLARK, JOSHUA HARRADINE, and EDMUND GROVER were indicted for feloniously assaulting Robert Palmer, on the 15th of March, with intent to rob him; against the Statute.
ROBERT PALMER. I am a sweep. On the 15th of March I was at Dalston, and saw the prisoners - I was going home to Plough-lane, Homerton; Grover, who was three or four yards behind the others, spoke to me - they were going the same road; I overtook them - Grover asked me where I was going; I told him to Homerton-I wished to persevere forward, but he began telling me his wife had lived servant at a house which I knew; I wished him good night, and went on, but had not got far before Clark hit me a blow, and down I went - I suppose I had got within a hundred yards of Church-street when I received the blow right on my forehead; Clark came rather in front of me - I fell down, and during the skirmish I felt his hand in my pocket; with that, to stand in my own defence, I had at him - after I had been at Clark then the other young man came and pitched into me directly; I was beaten and hammered about by them - the Policeman came up, and I gave charge of Clark, but he made a rush from the Policeman, and struck at me again; I was sober - I did not lose any money.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310407-205]
900. FANNY BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February, 1 watch, value 5l., the goods of George Edmonds, from his person.
GEORGE EDMONDS. I live at Stepney. On the 28th of February I was going home, and got to Stepney-green at a quarter-past ten o'clock at night; the prisoner accosted me, and asked how I did - I said I wanted nothing at all to say to her, as I was going home; she approached me, and made a snatch, as if at my watch - it was a French one, and a flat one; I felt it was gone - she ran off with all the speed she could, and I after her; she turned up a street just by the green, and I lost sight of her for a moment - I was passing up the street, and a gentleman said, "Are you running after any person? I said, "Yes, after a woman who has taken my watch;" he said, "She is gone up here, and can't make her escape - if you will stop here I will fetch a Policeman." which he did in two or three minutes - I went up with the Policeman, and found the prisoner in some rains; he said to her, "What have you done with the watch?""Watch! (says she,) I know nothing of any watch - you may search me if you like;" he looked about, and picked up the watch within a yard of her.
HUGH HUGHES. I am a Police-constable. I was called, and took the prisoner - she denied all knowledge of the watch; when I first saw her she was on her hands and knees, behind a heap of rubbish, covering something- I looked, and found this watch.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18310407-206]
OLD COURT. TUESDAY. APRIL 12.
Third Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
901. JOHN BLEASE was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of March, 1 jacket, value 5s.; 1 waistcoat, value 3s., and 1 pair of trousers, value 4s., the goods of Thomas George Mason.
THOMAS GEORGE MASON. I lodge in Buck's-row - the prisoner slept in the same room with me for about amonth. On the 7th of March, about a quarter-past seven o'clock, I went out, leaving my box locked, and the key in my pocket - I came back about six in the evening, and the prisoner was missing; my box was broken open, and my clothes gone - he never returned.
HONOR CULLIS. I slept in the next room to the prosecutor, who is apprenticed to my husband - it is usual for the lodger who last left the room to bring the key into our room; the prisoner brought it between eight and nine o'clock on the 7th of March - I put it on the shelf for about six hours; when I went to make the bed, I found a bed-screw under the quilt - the box appeared to have been broken open with that.
JOHN WILLIAMS. On the 31st of March I apprehended the prisoner and another boy who lodged in this room - they were discharged; Blease came to my house next morning, when I was out; he came a second time, and said he had come about the clothes - I said, "I thought you had done with that;" he said he came to tell me where they were, that he was the party who stole them, and said he did not confess at the office as he did not wish to implicate an innocent young man, and he would take me to where they were pawned, in the name of Smith - I went with him, and there we found them; he has got a bad father, but I believe bears a good character himself.
GEORGE PADDON. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Shoreditch. I have a jacket and trousers, pawned by the prisoner on the 7th of March.
(Property produced and sworn to)
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18310407-207]
902. WILLIAM SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of February, 7 1/2 lbs. of beef, value 3s., the goods of John Owen.
JOHN OWEN. I am a butcher, and have two shops in Fitzroy-market. On Saturday night, the 12th of February, I was coming to the shop, No. 1. - I was in a dark passage, and saw the prisoner and Gibson coming towards me; they came to my shop, held their coats over a piece of beef, and brought it away between them under their coats; Gibson had the beef at first, and gave it to the prisoner - I stopped them, and said, "What have you there?" they turned round, said nothing, but threw the beef down between them - I hallooed out, and a man picked it up; the prisoner was taken on Good Friday, which was six weeks after - I knew him before; I had a warrant to search his house, but he had left it - Gibson has been tried.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Do you mean to say you did not see him till Good Friday? A. I saw him once, but was too far off to get him; he laughed at me, and ran away out of Wylie's house - I had nobody to mind my shop while I followed him; I cannot swear I have not seen him three times at night in another dress - I might not know him - I saw him but once by day-light; I have known him four years; I very likely may have quarrelled with him - he was a witness for me here once, and said he could not swear to the man, though he had known him six years; this was about three years ago, when my shop was broken open - he has been impudent to me in the street.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310407-208]
903. CORNELIUS QUINLAN was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of March, 1 scuttle, value 3s., and 1 saucepan, value 6d., the goods of Robert Ford.
ELLEN FORD. I am the wife of Robert Ford - I had the care of a house, and a few odd things were given to me. On the 5th of March the prisoner was going by, crying old bottles, &c. - I called him in, and sold him some bottles; he offered me 3 1/2d. alb. for the scuttle, he said he live I in Milbourn-court, and if I would trust him he would take it home, weigh it, and pay me; I trusted him with it, but he has not paid me.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310407-209]
904. MARY McCARTHY was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of March, 1 waistcoat, value 7s., and 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of William Farrey.
WILLIAM FARREY. I am a seaman. On the 26th of March I was rather drunk, and met the prisoner - she went to show me where I could get a bed; I undressed, and put my clothes on the table - she wanted to come to bed to me; I would not let her - I wanted to go to sleep; she got on the bed - I kicked her off, and fell asleep directly; I had given her nothing - I awoke in the morning, and she was gone, also my waistcoat and handkerchief; if I gave them to her it is more than I know - I paid the landlady 1s. for the bed, and do not think I had more money about me but 6d., which I still found in my pocket.
JOHN THOMAS. I am an officer. I took the prisoner on the night of the 26th of March - I asked if she knew any thing of the waistcoat and handkerchief; she said the prosecutor had given them to her to pawn for 3s. 6d., for going with him, and she had done so.
EDWARD CHILD. I am a pawnbroker. On the 26th of March the prisoner pawned this waistcoat and handkerchief.
Prisoner's Defence. He gave me them to pawn.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310407-210]
905. BENJAMIN BIRCH was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of February, 4 pairs of boot-tops, value 22s., and 1 piece of patent leather, value 6d., the goods of Abraham Northen.
PHOEBE READ. I live in White Lion-yard, and am a shoe-maker. I have known the prisoner about three years- in February last he gave me two pairs of boot-tops to sell for him; I gave them to King, the officer.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Did you buy them yourself? A. No - I never knew any harm of him.
JOHN READ. I am the last witness' husband. The prisoner left two other pairs of boot-tops with me to sell; I did not sell them, but gave them to King.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you recollect when this was? A. Two or three days before the officer came, and a day or two after he left the two with my wife.
JOHN THOMAS KING. I am an officer. I received four pairs of boot-tops from Read.
ROWLAND SWAN. I am a serjeant of the Police. I saw the prisoner at the station-house; he said he did not deny taking the property - I searched his room, and found some leather.
ABRAHAM NORTHEN. I am a shoe-maker, and live in King-street, Smithfield. The prisoner has worked for me ten years; I missed four pairs of boot-tops on the 22nd or24th of February - these are them; they are called boottops in the trade.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor and Jury.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18310407-211]
906. MARY MARTIN was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of March, 1 half-crown, 1 sixpence, 1 penny-piece, and 1 farthing, the monies of Julias Von Boeck; 4 gallons of porter, value 5s., and 1 gallon of gin, value 9s., the goods of James Merry.
EUSTACE VON BOECK. I am the wife of Julius Von Boeck, who is a captain in the army, on half-pay. I deal with Merry, the publican, for beer and liquor; the prisoner was my servant - I occasionally gave her money to pay bills for liquor; she gave me the bills and receipts, but I have only kept the last. On Wednesday, the 12th of March, I gave her 3s. 1 1/2d. to pay this bill (looking at it); she brought me a receipt for it - (looking at two bills) these things were never delivered to me; I never ordered them, nor had them - I found the bills on the prisoner; she had lived about five months with me - I never sent her for these things.
The bills being read, were for beer and spirits, and amounted to 8s. 4 1/2d., and 14s. 4 1/2d.
JAMES MERRY. I keep a public-house. The last witness dealt with me; I know nothing of this bill, which has the receipt to it - it is not my hand-writing; the money was never paid me - these two bills are my writing; the prisoner had the beer and spirits charged in them on account of her mistress; I delivered the bills to the prisoner, and have never been paid; the receipt to the other bill is not written by me, or any of my servants.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that she had got spirits for herself, without so stating to Mr. Merry; and that at times when her mistress gave her money to pay the publican, she had laid it out for house-keeping.
GUILTY, of stealing 3s. 1 1/2d. only. Aged 28.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18310407-212]
907. LEWIS MYERS was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, 1 ladle, value 10s.; 13 spoons, value 30s.; 1 pepper-box, value 15s., and 1 pair of silver tongs, value 5s., the goods of Henry Myers.
HENRY MYERS. I am a stationer and rag-merchant, and live in White-street, Houndsditch. The prisoner is my brother; I went to Liverpool on the 27th of February, and left him living in the house - I am single; my mother and sisters live with me; he lived with them at that time; when I returned, I found he had absconded, and missed the articles named in the indictment - he had no authority whatever to pawn my property - he got his living by minding my warehouse in August last, but in February I hired a ship to send him abroad.
Prisoner. Q. Have you never sent me to Barker's to pawn property? A. I never had occasion to pawn things, I have always had plenty of money - I know nothing about my mother sending you to pledge for her.
THOMAS WHITLOCK. I am a Policeman. I apprehended the prisoner on the 3rd of March, in Henry-place, Waterloo-road, and found four duplicates on him.
JOHN HALL. I am a pawnbroker. I produce a soup ladle, 6 table-spoons, a pair of sugar-tongs, and a pepper-box, pawned by the prisoner on the 1st of March - he said it was for his brother (not the prosecutor); he said his brother had a bill to make up - I knew him well.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 18.
The prosecutor here stated that he had been robbed before by the prisoner, and had equipped him for sea, at an expence of 100l.; that he left the vessel, and, upon promises of amendment, he had been prevailed on to take him into his employ, where he remained until he committed the robbery in question.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-213]
908. JAMES MARNER and ELIZA HARRIS, were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of March, 1 looking-glass, value 5s.; 1 bolster, value 3s.; 2 pillows, value 3d.; 1 blanket, value 2s., and 1 counterpane, value 3s., the goods of James Parker.
JAMES PARKER. I live in Riley-street, Cromer-street; the prisoners lodged about five weeks with me in one room, and owed me a week's rent. On the 15th of March I went into their room; Harris said her husband had gone out - I said, "I have come to see if my things are all right;" she said, "They are not;" I said, "Where is the looking-glass?" she said it was in pawn - I then missed the other things - she was taken to the station; the man passed the house soon after - I went out and said,"You are a pretty sort of a man, to rob me of my things, even the looking-glass;" he said, "Well, I knew the glass was gone."
GEORGE NORRIS. I am an officer. I took the man in charge - he said he consented to the glass being pawned, but knew nothing of the other things.
JOSEPH HALL. I am a pawnbroker, and live at Battle-bridge. I have a bolster and blanket pawned by the female prisoner.
FRANCIS HENRY PARKER. I am a pawnbroker, and live at Somers'-town. The female prisoner pawned this looking-glass on the 14th of March.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Marner's Defence. I was aware of the glass, but did not intend to defraud him - I went out the morning I was taken to get some money to redeem them.
Harris' Defence. I can only say the same.'
MARNER - GUILTY. Aged 27.
HARRIS - GUILTY. Aged 25.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18310407-214]
909. ROBERT GOBBY was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of March, 1 coat, value 22s., and 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of George Terry.
GEORGE TERRY. I am barman to my aunt. On the 7th of March I went into the skittle-ground of the Britannia, Back-lane, St. George's in the East, and put my coat on a table in the ground; the handkerchief was in the pocket - I saw the prisoner there, and missed the coat in about three-quarters of an hour; he was then gone; he came in while I was looking for it - I went into the house, and inquired who had been out; I apprehended the prisoner on the 10th - the landlady found the duplicate of the coat in the sink of her beer-machine - I redeemed it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. I believe you offered to let the prisoner go if he gave you what you paid to redeem the coat? A. I did - I did not play atskittles with him after I lost my coat, and do not think I played with him that night.
GEORGE KETTERES. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Sun-street, Bishopsgate. This coat and handkerchief were delivered to the prosecutor on the 10th - it was pawned by the prisoner on the 8th of March, about nine o'clock in the morning; he said it was his own - that he worked at the docks; I talked to him for some time, and knew him.
The prisoner delivered in a petition for mercy, and received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Strongly Recommended to Mercy.
Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18310407-215]
910. FORTUNATO MARIE PEREIRA was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of March, 2 pairs of shoes, value 20s.; 1 pair of ear-rings, value 10s.; 1 handkerchief, value 2s., and 1 opera-glass, value 5s., the goods of Samuel Astell.
MARY ASTELL. I am the wife of Samuel Astell, of Wilstead-street, Somers'-town, builder. We have known the prisoner a year and a half - he lodged with us twice; the last time for four months; he owed us nearly thirty pounds - he went out in February last to go to his attorney, as he said; I missed these things while he was with us.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. I believe he is a Portuguese, who was obliged to fly from Don Miguel? A. Yes; he paid us honestly as long as he had money; and being distressed, I believe my husband at times gave him leave to pledge a few things, and he has redeemed them - he might have intended to redeem these.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310407-216]
911. PHILIP BURGIN was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of March, 1 carboy, value 7s., and 84lbs. of turpentine, value 2l. 9s., the goods of George Austin.
GEORGE AUSTIN. I live in Perceval-street, Clerkenwell. On the evening of the 10th of March this carboy of turpentine was taken from my door - I found it at the station-house next evening.
JAMES CHAMBERS. I am a Policeman. On the Friday Davy came to the station-house, and said he had found this property over night - I went to his house, and brought it from there; he is since deceased - his body was found on Saturday, previous to the Grand Jury sitting, and an inquest have returned a verdict of wilful murder against some persons unknown.
CHARLES SCOTCHMER. I am a Policeman. I apprehended the prisoner on the 11th of March; he said he knew what it was about, for he had heard about it, and that the night before he was coming from the doctor's, and met a man, who asked if he knew where Davy lived - that he took him there, and told him he had brought the man who owned the turpentine, but that he had had nothing more to do with it.
Prisoner's Defence. What the witness has said last is entirely false.
NOT GUILTY.
Second Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18310407-217]
912. WILLIAM FINCH was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, three handkerchiefs, value 7s., the goods of George Howard.
GEORGE HOWARD. I am a hosier, and live in Oxford-street. On the 1st of March the prisoner was brought into the shop, accused of stealing these handkerchiefs, which I had seen safe five minutes before - they were partly within the door-way.
JAMES KENNEDY. I am a music-copier, and live in Dover-street, Oxford-street. I was in Oxford-street, on the opposite side to Mr. Howard's-shop, and saw the prisoner, with another about four years older than himself, lurking about this shop; his companion crossed over the road - I looked towards the shop door; the other then went over, and took from the door-post the handkerchiefs - they were in one piece; the prisoner stood close at his side, held up his apron, which the other put them into, and both ran away - I ran over, and laid hold of the prisoner; the other quickened his pace, and got away - I took the prisoner within four doors of the shop, without losing sight of him; I took the handkerchiefs out of his apron, and took him into the shop.
WILLIAM SIBLEY. I am a Policeman. I received the prisoner in charge, with the handkerchiefs.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming along Oxford-street, and saw this boy with the handkerchiefs in his hand; he gave them to me - I asked where he got them; he made no answer, and I was taken.
GUILTY. Aged 12. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-218]
913. JAMES REEVES and JAMES JAY were indicted for stealing, on the 9th of March, 105lbs. of lead, value 8s., the goods of Nathaniel Gould, and fixed to a certain building.
SECOND COUNT, stating it to belong to William Cox and Thomas Cox.
CHARLES MATTHEWS. I am an assistant overseer at the Houseless Poor Asylum, in Playhouse-yard, Whitecross-street, St. Luke's - William and Thomas Cox are owners of the premises. On the 9th of March, about eleven o'clock in the morning, Attfield gave me information - the prisoners were then in custody; I went on the roof, and all the lead was stripped from the trap-door - the space is about five feet six inches, by three feet four inches; Attfield brought it on the 11th, and we compared it to the place - it fitted by two nails, which had been driven through into a board, which I have here - I have not a doubt of its belonging to that place; the prisoners were in the asylum for nine or ten weeks, as helpers and stokers - I saw them there on the 9th of March.
WILLIAM ATTFIELD. I am a constable of Worship-street. I stopped the prisoners about half-past nine o'clock that morning, about one hundred and fifty yards from the asylum - they were both carrying a load, which appeared very heavy, wrapped in a jacket and a pair of trousers; they turned into Cowhell-alley - one went into a Chimney-sweeper's there, and I pushed the other in, and with great difficulty took them to the office - they had got this lead; I went to the asylum, and on the 11th I saw the lead compared to the roof; it fitted the vacancy exactly - I observed this board; the holes in the lead corresponded with it - I have not a doubt the lead was taken from there; they said they brought the lead from home, but refused to say where.
JOSEPH GRAY. I am an assistant to the superintendent ofthe asylum. I saw the prisoners there on the 9th of March, between eight and nine o'clock in the morning - they have lived there ever since the 20th of December; the officer came to the asylum, about half-past eleven o'clock - I went to Worship-street, and saw the lead; I saw it compared with the roof; it fitted the vacancy exactly - I have not a doubt of its being taken from there.
CHARLES SCOTCHMER. I am a Policeman. Attfield called me to assist in securing the prisoners - the lead was then in the chimney-sweeper's house; we tied one of each of their hands together - one of them tried to untie it; he said,"What are you going to do with us? you can do nothing; where is the prosecutor? where is the man that owns it?" the lead was in two parcels, and weighs 105lbs. - I saw it applied to the roof; it completely fitted the vacancy.
Reeves put in a written Defence, stating that he was desired by an inmate at the asylum, who he did not know, to take the lead out for him; that he met Jay, and got him to assist.
Jay's Defence. I went out, and was coming back - I met Reeves, who asked me to go with him; I asked him several times how he came by the lead - he never gave me any satisfaction.
REEVES - GUILTY. Aged 21.
JAY - GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-219]
914. SARAH LACEY was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of December, 1 gown, value 4s., the goods of Robert Purser.
CAROLINE CLARISSA PURSER. I am the wife of Robert Purser, a pawnbroker, of Limehouse-causeway. This gown was pawned with us by Thomas, on the 13th of December for 3s., and on the 24th of December she sent a boy for it - the prisoner was in the shop at the time, redeeming something, and the boy put the things all on the counter together - she had left the shop about an hour before Thomas' gown was missed; Wiggins brought it to our shop on the 15th of March - I only knew the prisoner as a customer.
EDWARD WILLIAM WIGGINS. I am foreman to Mr. Dakers, of Limehouse. On the 14th of March the prisoner pawned this gown for 2s. 6d. - I saw her at the Police-station next day.
ANN THOMAS. This is my gown - I had pawned it at Purser's; on the 24th of December I sent my boy to redeem it; he returned without it.
HENRY LEE. I am a Policeman. I apprehended the prisoner at the Steam Washing Company's premises, Millwall - she said she had bought the gown in Salmon-lane.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that she had bought the gown.
GUILTY. Aged 48. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18310407-220]
915. JOHN HILL, MICHAEL ARTHUR, and THOMAS GODDARD were indicted for stealing, on the 17th of March, 11 yards of stuff, value 20s.; 3 yards of cotton, value 2s.; 2 pairs of socks, value 6d.; 1 pair of shoes, value 1s.; 1 band, value 1s., and 2 frocks, value 2s., the goods of Mary Ann Watson.
MARY ANN WATSON. I am a widow, and live in Praed-street, Paddington, and keep a haberdasher's-shop. On the 17th of March, at ten o'clock in the morning, I saw these goods safe in my shop - I went out; I went about seven in the evening to Edgware-road, and, between eight and nine, I received information that the shop had been robbed - I went there, and missed these articles; some of them were found in the street - I was fetched next morning to the Police-station, and saw some things like mine - I thought they might be mine, but I was out at the time the shop was robbed; these things are really so very common I cannot identify any of them - the stuff is very common.
WILLIAM HANCOCK. I am a Policeman. On the 17th of March, about ten minutes past seven o'clock, I saw the three prisoners at the prosecutrix's window - I was in plain clothes, as a patrol; I saw Arthur at the window, the other two were close by him - I could not see whether he took any thing; Goddard stood in front, and Hill stood behind him - they went away from the window; Arthur went on the other side of the way - I collared him, then went into some empty houses, and brought the others out - I found this band in Arthur's hat - Mrs. Watson swore to that.
JOHN EVANS. I am a Policeman. About ten minutes past seven o'clock I saw Arthur on the opposite side to Watson's house, looking towards some empty houses; I secured him.
MRS. WATSON. These things are so very common I cannot swear to them; I lost things like them.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310407-221]
916. JOHN HUGHES was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of February, 1 handkerchief, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Joseph William Hornby, from his person.
JOSEPH WILLIAM HORNBY. I live in Great Cambridge-street, Hackney-road. On the 19th of February, about seven o'clock in the evening, I was in Old-street, going into the City - I wanted to use my handkerchief, and on feeling in my pocket it was gone; while I was feeling for it an officer came up, and asked if I had lost any thing - he went about fifteen yards forward, and caught the prisoner, brought him back, and said, "This is the boy who picked your pocket, I saw him;" somebody in the crowd told him not to be afraid - he said, "I have done nothing, what have I to be afraid of?" my handkerchief has not been found.
GEORGE KEMP. I am a Policeman. On the 19th of February, I saw Mr. Hornby near the London Apprentice- I saw the prisoner in company with another boy, lay hold of Mr. Hornby's coat pocket; I was in plain clothes - he turned round, saw me, and I suppose knew me, for he let go of it; I crossed over to the other side, saw him following Mr. Hornby, watched him, and near the City-road, I saw the prisoner put his hand into the pocket, take the handkerchief out, and give it to the other, who ran away with it directly - I informed Mr. Hornby, and fetched the prisoner back, without losing sight of him; I had seen him before - I think I should know the other lad.
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Respited.
[trialAccount t18310407-222]
917. THOMAS GRIFFITHS, JOHN STANFORD, JAMES GANDER, and THOMAS KELLY were indicted for stealing, on the 24th of February, 1 cap, value 9s., the goods of Hugh McMahon, the elder, from the person of Hugh McMahon , the younger.
ELIZABETH McMAHON. I am the wife of Hugh McMahon, a journeyman tailor - we live in Camden-street, Camden-town; my son Hugh is eight years old. On the 24th of February, between seven and eight o'clock in theevening, I saw him standing at the door, with the cap on his head - I did not see him go from the door myself; it was a cloth cap, worth 8s. or 9s. - I met him crying in the street in about ten minutes; he complained that some boys who passed the door had dropped a halfpenny, and asked if he would have it - that when he stopped to pick it up, they snatched his cap off, and ran away with it; I have since seen the peak of it, and am certain it is the peak of his cap - I saw the prisoners in custody in about ten minutes, and heard them accusing each other of having taken the cap off the child's head.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q.And, of course, they each denied it? A. Yes, I rather think so - I know nothing of a hat being found.
HUGH McMAHON. I live with my parents. I was standing at the door, and saw some boys - I recollect five boys; I cannot recollect whether the prisoners were part of them - the boys took the cap off my head, and ran away with it.
Cross-examined. Q. Did the boy who took the cap put another on your head? A. Yes, but he took that off again.
JOHN BURK. I live in King's-road, near to Mrs. McMahon. On the 24th of February I saw the last witness in Camden-street, Camden-town, standing outside the door on the flag-stone - I saw the four prisoners, and another boy named Batcheler, hustle him, pushing him about; I cannot swear whether he had any thing on his head, as it was rather dusk - I did not see them do any thing but run away, after hustling him; I saw him again in about ten minutes, and assisted in apprehending Kelly and Stanford.
Cross-examined. Q. What took you to the spot? A. I was going on an errand - I live with my father, and sell fruit about the street; I was not acquainted with Batcheler - I know all the prisoners perfectly well; I did not see the cap taken.
WILLIAM PRICE. I am a Policeman. On the 24th of February, at near eight o'clock at night, I heard the call of Police, and took Batcheler and Stanford - Burk secured two more; the whole five were together - I charged them with stealing the cap, having seen Mrs. McMahon; I saw one of them throw what appeared to me to be a cap into the road, but I could not secure it - I took Batcheler, Stanford, and Kelly to the station-house; I afterwards went and took he other two from Batcheler's information, as he said, as we went along, "If you won't hurt me I will tell you who stole the cap, and where it is;" I took the others to the station, and went back with him - he showed me where they had thrown the cap, and I found on Griffiths a knife, which the cap had been cut with; the cap had been cut, for the peak, which has been found, has part of the leather fastened to it - I only found the peak where Batcheler took me; it was thrown down an area.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you take on yourself to say that Griffiths cut the cap? A. No, but he is the only one who had the knife - when I took Batcheler he had Kelly's old cap on; I found his own cap next morning in a field- Batcheler did not say he had taken the cap himself.
MRS. McMAHON. I swear this is the peak of my boy's cap; it is leather - I know it, because I beat him severely for breaking it at school; this is broken in two places, the same as his was.
Cross-examined. Q. That accident might happen to any other cap? A. I know this is the peak - my husband made it himself, and there are a great many more holes in it than necessary, as he did not understand the mode of putting it on.
WILLIAM BATCHELER. I am nearly fourteen years old. I was in company with the prisoners on the 24th of February, and saw Mrs. McMahon's son outside the door Griffiths said to him, "I have lost a halfpenny down here, and if you like to find it you may keep it for yourself;" he stooped to look for it, and Griffiths snatched off his cap and put his own on the boy's head - he then took his own off his head again, and ran away; the boy's cap looked like a new one - the other three prisoners were with him at the time; he gave his cap to Kelly, who said, "I am d - d if I don't wear this cap;" I said, "Kelly, mine is a poor old cap, you may as well give me yours;" I saw all this done.
Q. What did you say? A. They were going up higher to steal a till - they had a leg of mutton, and a batter pudding the night before; I told him to give the cap back to the boy, but he would not - I was taken in a quarter of an hour, and told of this directly.
Q. Why not go and tell Mrs. McMahon? A. They all ran away, and I with them - Griffiths cut the peak off the cap with an iron handled knife, he chucked it down an area, and then put it on Kelly's head; I have known the prisoners about three months, but did not keep company with them - I live with my parents; my father sells watercresses, and I go about with him.
Cross-examined. Q. You went about every day with water-cresses? A. Except being in the House of Correction for a month with Stanford, when they took him, and me for being with him - he has been there sixteen times; I was never there but once - I did not keep up an acquaintance with him: I was going out to call my brother to tea this day, and I met him - we had not been out of prison two hours then - they made me go with them, for Griffiths snatched off my cap to make me come, and said he was going to get a batter pudding; it was not my cap that was put on the boy's head - I meant to tell the first officer I met of it; I was always brought up honest - Griffiths only threw the peak into the area; he threw the cap into an old iron-shop - I took the officer to the shop in half an hour, but he could not find it; I got my cap back in the morning.
Stanford's Defence. I deny all Batcheler has said - he did not go to find the cap till the morning.
Kelly's Defence. He said he would go and nick the cap, and we ran on; when he went home he asked Griffiths to lend him his knife, and he cut the peak off - he knocked my cap off, then picked it up, and said my cap was on his head.
Gander's Defence. Batcheler said, "Here is a half-penny for you," and when the boy stopped, he knocked off his hat, and ran away.
Griffiths, Gander, and Kelly received a good character.
STANFORD - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Transported for Seven Years.
GRIFFITHS - GUILTY. Aged 13.
GANDER - GUILTY. Aged 9.
KELLY - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Judgment Respited .

919. WILLIAM GEORGE SHARP was indicted for a misdemeanor.
HENRY STOKES. I am a meat-salesman at Newgate-market. On the 7th of February the prisoner came to me, and said he wanted a leg of mutton, or pork, for Mr. Knock, of Well-street, who is a customer of mine; we let him have a leg of mutton, believing he came from Mr. Knock, whose apprentice I knew he had been.
JOHN KNOCK. I am a bookbinder, and deal with Mr. Stokes. The prisoner was my apprentice for twelve months, and I used to send him for meat; he was not in my employ on the 7th of February - I did not send him for this meat; he never brought it to me - he had absconded from me a month.
MARIA KNOCK. I am Mr. Knock's wife. I did not authorize the prisoner to go to Mr. Stokes' for meat - he never brought it.
Prisoner's Defence. I never went there after I left Mr. Knock.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18310407-225]
920. THOMAS SMITH and ANN SMITH were indicted for a misdemeanor.
MARIA HOWARD. I am a servant, and live in Leonard-street. On the 29th of March, about five o'clock, I overtook the prisoners in Newgate-street - they were quite strangers to me; I only saw the male prisoner at first; they were not walking together - he was stooping when I overtook him, and said he had picked something up; he went on, stopped, and I overtook him again three or four yards further; then he showed me some earrings; he went on to the corner of Newgate-street - he had not walked above a dozen yards altogether when he showed a parcel to me; he said he had picked them up, and was never so lucky in his life before as to pick any thing up of any value - I said, "Have you?" he then crossed over to the New Post-office, and near the Bull and Mouth, St. Martin's-le-grand, I saw him again on the same side of the way as I was on; he showed me an ear-ring and ring - I had seen the ring before; he asked me to read the bill for him; they were tied in paper; I read it - the amount was 5l. 18s. 6d., it was for a pair of ear rings and two gold rings - we gave it to the Magistrate at Guildhall, and it has not been returned- he asked if I would buy them, and said I should have them for 1l.; that he was in distress, and a little money would be more useful to him than the parcel - (I did not see the female prisoner till the man was taken into custody;) I said I could not, and then he said I should have them for 15s.; I gave him 14s. 6d. for them - he then left me, and I did not see him any more till he was taken into custody - I took the ear-rings and rings; the officer, who had been watching, came up, and asked if I had bought any thing; I delivered them to him - he took both the prisoners into custody.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Did you not at first deny to the officer that you had bought any thing? A. Yes; I then walked on five or six yards; the officer spoke to me again - I told him, and delivered them to him; I did not suppose I had done wrong; the prisoner stopped and spoke to me first.
ROBERT TYRRELL. I am an officer of the Post-office. On the 29th of March, about five o'clock in the afternoon, I saw the two prisoners in company, going backward and forward in Newgate-street, which excited my suspicion I saw the man follow the prosecutrix down Newgate-street, and show her something in a paper; he followed her to the end of Jewin-street, and suddenly parted from her - the female who was in his company kept on the opposite side; I told the street-keeper to call the prosecutrix back, and just at that moment the male prisoner crossed over, gave something to the female, and said,"Here is 14s. 6d.;" I took hold of them, pushed them into a house, and took the 14s. 6d. out of the female's hand - I am certain they were in company; she was very reluctant to give up the 14s. 6d., and dropped a ring out of her hand; it appeared a wedding-ring - I searched the male prisoner, and found in his waistcoat pocket a strip of paper, on which was written "Two pairs of gold ear-rings, two gold keys," amounting to 5l. 18s. 6d.; I gave that paper to the Magistrate, and it was lost - the street-keeper came back to me, and said the prosecutrix had bought nothing; I sent him back again; he brought her back, and said she had bought the ear-rings - I found a key in the male prisoner's pocket; I searched his lodgings in Green-street, Commercial-road, and on his shelf found another paper, with the same words on it.
Cross-examined. Q. You say they were together - had you your eyes on them the whole time? A. I cannot say that I had; I saw them for four or five minutes before he addressed the prosecutrix, and saw him speak to several young women, which excited my suspicion - he was standing when the prosecutrix passed him; he then went in front of her.
MARIA ANN COPELAND. I am matron of Giltspur-street Compter. When the female prisoner was brought in the officer requested me to search her - I took her into an adjoining room, and saw her put her hand to her bonnet I felt her bonnet, and said, "What have you here?" she said, "Nothing but curl-papers;" I said, "I insist on searching, for there is something more;" she was going to strike me - I turned to call the officer, and when I returned with him, she approached towards me, and said, "You may now search me," and I picked a paper off the ground, containing four rings.
JOSEPH HAWK RIGGE. I am a street-keeper. Tyrrell beckoned to me about the middle of Aldersgate-street - I followed the prosecutrix, and asked her to return with me, and if she had bought any thing - she said No, at first; she afterwards gave me the ear-rings and a mourning-ring- I have also those picked up in the prison, and have had them ever since.
HENRY LESTER. I am a jeweller. These ear-rings are brass, and not worth 1d. - the rings are brass, and worth nothing; I would not give 1 1/2d. for them all.
MR. BARRY (on behalf of the defendants) addressed the Court.
T. SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 40.
Transported for Seven Years.
A. SMITH - NOT GUILTY.

921. JOHN EVANS was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of February, 1 pair of shoes, value 5s., the goods of Galen Bamfield.
CHRISTOPHER BAMFIELD. I am the son of Galen Bamfield - he is a boot and shoe-maker, and lives in the Strand. On the 23rd of February, about five o'clock in the afternoon, the prisoner came in, and asked for a pair of boots I went outside the window to get him a boot; I saw him take these shoes, and put them into his pocket - I came in, and he said the boots I showed him were too light, he wanted strong ones; he went out, and I called Stop thief! he was taken without my losing sight of him, and the shoes found in his coat pocket.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that the shoes found on him, he had just taken out of pledge.
C. BAMFIELD. It is not true - I saw him take them off the shop-board.
GUILTY. Aged 26. - Confined Three Months.

922. JOSEPH DANIELS was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of March, 1 apron, value 1s. 3d., the goods of Peter Puttock.
GEORGE BRODIE. I live in Chapel-street, just opposite Mr. Puttock's. On the 22nd of March, about five o'clock, I saw the prisoner looking in at the window - I saw him snatch an apron off the eggs in the shop, and run away; I saw him running, but could not catch him - I had seen him several times before, and knew him perfectly well; I saw him in custody the next day.
CHARLES CLARK. I apprehended the prisoner in a yard, among a parcel of boys - I knew him well by the description given of him; when he saw me he ran into a house, and tried to hide himself - I took him, and he said,"What do you want?" I said, "You will soon know," and as we went along he said, "I know nothing about the apron;" I had not mentioned an apron.
PETER PUTTOCK. I was at home, and missed the apron, but did not see it taken.
GUILTY. Aged 13. - Transported for Seven Years.

924. ELIZABETH CRESWICK was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of March, 1 coat, value 10s.; 1 pair of breeches, value 2s.; 3 shirts, value 6s.; 1 pair of stockings, value 1s. 6d.; 2 waistcoats, value 2s., and 1 umbrella, value 1s., the goods of James Goodhall.
JAMES GOODHALL. I live in Wilson-street, Somers'-town. On the 22nd of March the prisoner was cleaning out a room at my house, and at night, when she was gone, I missed a sheet off the bed, a coat out of a box, and the breeches off the table; she was my char-woman - I have known her two years: I never allowed her to pawn my property.
JAMES LIMBIRD. I apprehended the prisoner - she gave me the duplicates of the property.
GEORGE WADE. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Charlton-street, Somers'-town. I have all the articles stated in the indictment, which were pawned by the prisoner, at different times.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 42. - Confined Three Months.

925. CHARLES BRINKLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of March, 1 jewel-case, value 40s., and 1 flute, value 10s., the goods of Samuel Starkey.
SAMUEL STARKEY. I live in Queen-square, Aldersgate-street. On the 24th of March, between three and four o'clock, a person whom I employ out of doors, sent his son with some work; he rang the bell - the boy answered it; he said he would wait five minutes for an answer - during that time somebody entered the parlour, and took this case and flute; my boy went down, found the parlour door open, and gave an alarm - I went out, and caught the prisoner within five minutes, in Peter's-lane, St. John-street, with the jewel-case under his arm, covered with a handkerchief; I found my flute in his trousers pocket - he was quite a stranger.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOHN ABRAHALL. I am the prosecutor's errand boy - the bell rang; I came down to a boy, and left the door open, but I did not see the prisoner - I knew the flute was in the parlour.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming across Smithfield - a gentleman came up, and asked me to carry these things to the Angel, at Islington - I thought he was behind me at the time.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Transported for Seven Years.

926. GEORGE AUSTIN was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of March, 6 silver spoons, value 20s., the goods of William Michie.
WILLIAM MICHIE. I keep the Spread Eagle, in Oxford-street . The prisoner is quite a stranger to me. On the 21st of March, between ten and eleven o'clock, I missed these spoons off the sideboard in the coffee-room - there were eleven on the sideboard, but only six were taken; they were in the waiter's charge - I had not seen the prisoner in the house.
HYLA BETTS. I am a refiner of gold and silver, and live in Long-acre. On the 23rd of March the prisoner offered these spoons for sale, cut into pieces; I suspected he had not come honestly by them, and detained him; he said they were given him by a friend to dispose of - I sent for a Policeman, and gave him in charge.
THOMAS HEYGATE. I am a Policeman. I received the prisoner in charge, with the property.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming over the water, and met a friend in the Blackfriars-road; I went home with her, and after waiting some time, (I was tipsy) I received them from her - she put into my hand a paper; I did not knowwhat it was till morning; when I found what I had got, I thought they were of no use, and she intended them for sale; I know nothing of the robbery.
GUILTY. Aged 29. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-232]
927. ROBERT WESTWICK, alias MITCHELL , was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of March, 2 shoes, value 2s., the goods of William Hayhan.
JOHN JENKINS. I am in the employ of William Hayhan; he is a shoemaker, and lives in Ratcliff-highway. On the 2nd of March, a person said a man had taken some shoes - I went out, and found the prisoner about twenty doors off I said, "You have stolen my master's shoes" - he said he had not - I told him he had - he then pulled them from under his jacket; they were master's, and had been hanging at our door.
Prisoner's Defence I was passing the shop - I picked up the shoes a yard and a half from the prosecutor's door; I carried them publicly in my hand to see who would own them, till I got near Old Gravel-lane; I then put them under my jacket - this man asked me for them, and I gave them to him.
GUILTY. Aged 60. - Confined Three Weeks.
[trialAccount t18310407-233]
928. THOMAS POSTON was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, 4 pieces of carpet, value 3s., and 40 pieces of candle, value 6d., the goods of James Willis.
JAMES WILLIS. I keep Fladong's-hotel, Oxford-street . The prisoner was in my service twenty-two months, as porter, and was so on the 1st of March; many gentlemen complained of losing property - I got a search-warrant, and found on his premises a quantity of carpet, pieces of candle, and a number of duplicates of glass, which I believe to be my property; I have pieces of carpet similar to those found, and the candles are a peculiar make - I have missed candles from time to time.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. What is the peculiar make? A. They are made to fit candlesticks which I have - they are not a common length; I had them made short for the use of the bed-room; they are tallow - I had not sold any carpets - I had a man named Hill in my employ; I sold him some carpet once, but I sold none while the prisoner lived with me - when I said I had not sold any carpets, I meant while the prisoner had lived with me - some of these pieces are old, and some are much worn - I know nothing of the prisoner buying any carpet of Hill - I may have sold him six or seven patterns, or there might be twenty; these may be called remnants; they are cut up I took the prisoner on the recommendation of our head porter, Hill - I believe he had lived formerly with my predecessor, for four or five years.
EDWARD RURRIDGE. I am an officer, of High-street. I went to the prisoner's lodgings on the 14th of March; he was not at home, but his wife was - I found some of these carpets on the floor, and some on a chair - the candles were in a cupboard - I was called down stairs, and when I got up again, I found his wife had removed them into another room - I took the prisoner there between five and six o'clock next morning, and told him it was on suspicion of stealing various things from his master - he said, "My wife has told me all about it; I have not taken any thing" - he did not show the least fear.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310407-234]
929. JANE TAYLOR was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of March, 2 pewter pots, value 2s., the goods of George White.
JAMES PAKES. I am servant to George White, who keeps the Hope and Anchor, in Hereford-street . On the 8th of March, about eight o'clock in the evening, I was telling my brother what a quantity of pots had been stolen, and I observed the prisoner in the tap-room - I was standing at the door; she came out and stood by my side, and then shifted to the lower step - I heard something rattle, saw her stooping, and I went to her, and picked up a pot; where it came from I cannot tell - I took her to my master, and on looking down, saw another pot hanging below her clothes - he sent for an officer.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you take the pot from under her clothes? A. No; it hung below her clothes; I cannot say what it hung to; she was tipsy- she said it was tied to her gown - there were a set of fellows larking in the room - she complained it had been tied to her tail - I do not believe she meant to steal them.
NOT GUILTY.

930. JOSEPH PRAGNELL was indicted for embezzlement; to which he pleaded

[trialAccount t18310407-236]
931. CHARLES ALLINGTON was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of February, 2 fixtures, (i. e.) 2 coppers, belonging to Samuel Ridge, and fixed to a building; against the Statute, & c.
SAMUEL RIDGE. I live at Bishop Bonner's-hall, in the parish of Bethnal-green. On the 25th of February I missed two coppers from a house of mine in the neighbourhood, which I had let.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. I believe you had the misfortune to have a tenant named Kendall? A. I had - he ran away; I had my rent, because the goods were sold - the key was delivered up to me the day this bill was found; I do not know where Kendall is.
WILLIAM CASTELL. I was in a barn at Bonner's-hall on a Friday morning - I heard something; I went out, and saw the prisoner with two coppers on his back - he threw them down when he saw me, and ran off; I have seen them fitted to two places in the kitchen at Bonner's-hall, and they fit exactly; he was twenty or thirty yards from the house when I saw him - there are gates in front of the house, and they were open.
Cross-examined. Q.Do you know Kendall? A. I know he lived there - he is gone off; the door was open, but had not been broken.
DONALD McDONALD. I am a Police-constable. I was on Twig Folly-bridge, about four hundred yards from the prosecutor's - I heard a cry of Stop thief! and took the prisoner, who was running; I took him to Bonner's-hall, where the coppers were afterwards fitted.
Cross-examined. Q.Did he not tell you he was engaged to carry the coppers? A. Yes, that some person had the coppers on the ground, and said they would givehim 6d. to carry them across the field - nothing in the least suspicious was found on him.
WILLIAM BROOKS. I was in the yard at Mr. Ridge's- I heard the cry of Stop thief! I went about twenty yards in front of Mr. Ridge's, and saw two coppers laying in a ditch, about sixty or seventy yards from the place they were fitted to; I took them, and went and fitted them in the places - they fitted exactly.
Prisoner's Defence. A gentleman asked me to carry them across the field, and he was to give me 6d. for it - he ran away; I was frightened, and threw them down - the gentleman then called Stop thief! and I ran away; there was a man with the person who hired me, who called out Mr. Kendall - he was a tall man, with a blue coat and light breeches.
MR. RIDGE re-examined. Q. How long had Kendall been away? A. Since about Christmas - I have not seen him since; he had the key in his possession.
Q. How lately before these coppers were missed had there been a sale? A.Shortly after Christmas - I do not know whether the house had been empty; the brokers might have gone into it - it was not in my possession; Kendall was a gentlemanly man - he had the use of the fixtures, which had been left by a former tenant.
WILLIAM CASTELL. I saw two men with a truck, in a lane twenty or thirty yards from the prisoner - it was a truck that would have carried the coppers; there was a field - the prisoner was going towards the truck; it was going on - I did not notice the men, nor see what was in the truck.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310407-237]
932. JOHN BECKENHAM and JAMES WEBB were indicted for stealing, on the 16th of March, 1 bushel of oats, value 4s., and 1 sack, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Meredith.
WILLIAM GREGORY. I am a Police-constable. On the night of the 16th of March I was on duty in Praed-street, Paddington - I saw Beckenham coming down with a sack of corn, and he asked me if I could tell him which was No. 137; I said I thought he knew as well as I did, it was not at that end of the street; I then asked what he had in the sack - he said a bushel of corn which he was going to take from Mr. Meredith's wharf to Mr. Whitmore's, and if I went with him there I should find it was all right; I went with him, and Mr. Whitmore denied all knowledge of it - I then took him to the station, and in going down the street he said, "The whole of it is, it is stolen corn, and I had it from James Webb from Mr. Meredith's stable;" I went and informed Mr. Meredith, and he gave charge of Beckenham - I went and took Webb at Mr. Meredith's stable, No. 9 wharf, Paddington; he said, "I own I gave the corn to Beckenham to take to Mr. Whitmore's, and I hope my master will look over it;" he said he was to bring back 4s.
Beckenham. I said I was going to Whitmore's, but I am no scholar, and do not know the number; Whitmore said he had not ordered it, and I said I would go back and ask the master if he had ordered Webb to send it any where - if it was not ordered it must be stolen. Witness. No, he did not say that - the number he asked for is where he lives himself.
THOMAS MEREDITH. I have a stable at No. 9 wharf Paddington - Webb is my carman; I have seen these oats and sack - they are mine; my name is on the sack I have some of the same oats in my bulk; Beckenham was employed occasionally, and had been that day unloading coals - I believe I have been robbed to a great extent, and my horses have suffered; Webb has received money from time to time, and paid it over to me - I sell corn in small quantities.
Beckenham's Defence. I was completely out of work; I did not know these were stolen.
BECKENHAM - GUILTY. Aged 30.
WEBB - GUILTY. Aged 31.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18310407-238]
933. WILLIAM ANDERSON was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of March, 2 rings, value 16s., and 1 pair of ear-rings, value 15s., the goods of Elizabeth Rider.
