NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18310407-277]
NEW COURT. WEDNESDAY, APRIL 13.
Fifth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Sergeant.
972. JAMES SMITH was indicted for bigamy.
DANIEL HONE. I live in Castle-street, Bloomsbury - I am a hair-dresser. I went to the parish church of St. Martin in the Fields, with the prisoner, my wife, and another person - the prisoner was there married to my sister, Elizabeth Hone; she was at that time single - she is still alive; they lived together till last Spring - I think in April last he left her.
SUSAN TIBBS ROWEL. I live in George-street, Portman Chapel. I have known the prisoner two years last August - I understood him to be single, and that he was living in Marylebone-lane; I went to Christchurch, Spitalfields, with him on the 25th of last May - we were married there; the pew-opener and the clerk were present - I was married in the name of Susan Rowel; a friend of mine heard the prisoner was married, while I was in the country with him, at Overton, in Hampshire, and when I came to town, seven weeks or two months after we were married, I heard of it.
Prisoner. I was with her drinking in Tottenham-court-road, two or three days before I was married, I got nearly tipsy, and I told her I was married. Witness. No, he did not - I never knew it; I have been with him there several times, and have taken a glass of ale, but have never been intoxicated - he never told me he was married.
Prisoner. I told her the very day, that I had not money to provide for the marriage, and she had better put it off to a future time. Witness. I did pay for the marriage, but I never understood he wished to put it off - I was living in Carpenter's-buildings, London-wall, in service, up to the day of the marriage.
Prisoner. After we came to London she agreed to go to service; I got work at Cambridge - she heard where I was, and, as she was in the family-way, came down to me; being out of a situation I came to London, and she agreed to go to her aunt's - while she was there she was confined. Witness. I had a child on the 19th of January - I did not go to him without his sending for me.
Prisoner. I lived very comfortably with my first wife, and she proved a very good one till we removed to Westminster, when she got acquainted with a man named Mettel - she used to go out with him, and get intoxicated - she came to me, and took away the money I had to provide Counsel with, and left me without; she had this Susan Rowel with her at the time - she told me therewould he no trial, for they would keep away; I have no witnesses on that account.
GUILTY. Aged 36. - Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18310407-278]
973. WILLIAM GEORGE SHARP was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, 1 pair of graining-plates, value 1l., the goods of William Crawford.
WILLIAM CRAWFORD. I live in Walbrook-buildings, Hoxton, and am assistant to my father. Mr. William Crawford, of Peerless-row, a book-binder. On the 16th of February the prisoner came to my father, and asked for the loan of a pair of quarto morocco graining-plates for Mr. Knock - I lent them to him, and he never returned them.
GEORGE KNAPP. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Golden-lane. On the 16th of February the prisoner brought two pairs of graining-plates, which he said he had brought for his father, whose name was James Sanders, No. 13, Whitecross-street, and his own name was John Sanders.
JOHN KNOCK. The prisoner was my apprentice, and left me on the 23rd of January - I did not send him to Mr. Crawford's to borrow any thing on the 16th of February.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I hope you will be as favourable as you can.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
[trialAccount t18310407-279]
974. WILLIAM GEORGE SHARP was again indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, 1 pair of graining-plates, value 25s., the goods of Alexander Crawford.
ALEXANDER CHAWFORD. I live in Peerless-row. The prisoner came to my house on the 16th of February, and borrowed a pair of morocco graining-plates for Mr. Knock - I let him have them; these are them.
Prisoner. Q.Did you not say you gave me the plates? A. Yes, I gave them into your hand, as you stood at the door, for your master.
COURT. Q. You did not intend to part with the property? A.Certainly not, only to lend them to his master - he had borrowed them for him before.
GEORGE KNAPP. I took these plates in pawn with the others, and lent 2s. on them.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Three Months, and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18310407-280]
975. ELIZA HURRY was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of March, 3 half-crowns, and 1 crown, the monies of John Gardiner, from his person.
JOHN GARDINER. I live in Lamb-alley, Bermondsey-street, and am a skinner. On the 6th of March I went to a public-house in Wentworth-street - I had a pint or two of beer; I asked for a bed, and they could not let me have one, but recommended me to a private house just by - I went there, and went to bed; I counted what money I had before I went to bed; I had two crowns and three half-crowns, and I put them into my trousers pocket - there was a candle in the room; when I had been in bed about twenty minutes, I saw a woman come into the room with nothing on her but her shift - she came to my bed-side; I asked what she wanted - she said to come to bed to me; I had placed my trousers, with my money in them, under my head, but when I looked round I saw them on the floor, near to the prisoner - I got out of bed, examined my trousers pockets, and missed the three half-crowns and one of the 5s. pieces; I accused the prisoner of robbing me - she could not speak, on account of having something in her mouth; I seized her, and in the scuffle the candle went out - I called the people of the house, and sent for a Policeman; when he was coming up the stairs the prisoner put a crown-piece and a half-crown into my hand, and said she would make it all up before the Policeman came - I said I would not till she gave me the other two half-crowns; I saw she had something in her mouth - I seized her throat, and grappled with her, but I have never seen the other two half-crowns since; I detained her till the officer came in- I had had a pint or two of beer, just to refresh myself.
Prisoner. I was in bed when he came into the house; he asked if there was any woman in the house - he heard me cough, and said, "That is a woman's voice." Witness. No, I did not; I did not get out of bed and let her in in her shift.
COURT. Q.How came you to go there for a bed? A. I had been to some friends, then went to the public-house, and as I felt myself fatigued I thought I would get a bed there.
Prisoner. Did I not say, "If you think I have any money, where have I got to put it?" and then you asked the woman to send for an officer; I did not offer to go away - I did not put this money into your hand. Witness. Yes, you put the money in my hand in the dark.
JURY. Q.What hour was it? A. A quarter before eight o'clock - I had never seen the prisoner before, and did not know she was in the house till she came to my bed-side; I suspected it was a house of ill-fame when I got into it, and that made me count my money - I never was in the house before; I am not married.
JAMES WALTON. I am a Police-constable. On the night of the 6th of March I was in Wentworth-street; I was called, and went to the house, where I found the prisoner and the prosecutor on the second floor - the prosecutor was standing in his shirt, and the prisoner in her shift; I took a light with me - there was no light in the room; the prosecutor said she had robbed him of 12s. 6d. - he held out his hands, and said, "Here is what she has given me back," holding a crown-piece in one of his hands, and a half-crown in the other - I took the prisoner to the station-house, but found nothing on her; the prosecutor appeared sensible of what was going on - there was a partition between the room his bed was in and the other; that house makes up from seventy to eighty beds- they are all in narrow slips; the partitions only go up about six or seven feet high - it is more like a rabbit-warren than any thing else; any person might throw any thing over the partition; I have had several charges from that house.
JURY. Q. What is the sign of the public-house? A. The Two Brewers.
HENRY MARQUARD. I was with Walton - I received the crown and half-crown; we could not find any more -I did not observe that there had been a candle in the room, which had been put out.
Prisoner's Defence. He had been drinking - heowned himself he was drinking with another female at the corner.
JOHN GARDINER. It is false.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-281]
976. CHARLES PROUTINGS was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of April, 7 books, value 4s. 6d., the goods of John Francis Tappin and William Cousans.
WILLIAM COUSANS, I am in partnership with John Francis Tappin; we are booksellers, and live in High-street, Marylebone. On the 5th of April the constable came to our house, and brought some books, which I knew- we have a stall-board in front of our house, on which I had placed, among other books, Guy's grammar that morning, about eight o'clock - the officer brought that and the other books about two o'clock; they had not been sold.
WILLIAM HUGHES. I am a Police-constable. I saw the prisoner in Queen-street, Edgware-read on the 5th of April - he had a parcel tied up in a handkerchief; I stopped him, and asked him what he had got - he said some books which he had bought of two boys in Paddington-street, and given 1s. 3d. for them - he was then about a quarter of a mile from the prosecutor's.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I knew nothing of their being stolen - I bought them of two boys for 1s. 3d.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18310407-282]
977. MICHAEL MCLOUGHLIN was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of April, 1 half-crown, the money of Mary Bruce, from her person.
MARY BRUCE. I live in the Almonry, Westminster. On the night of the 6th of April I met the prisoner coming up the passage into the Almonry - he asked me to go with him, but I would not; he then asked me to go and have some supper - we went to a cook-shop, in Tothill-street; I sat down by him, and while I was sitting I felt his hand in my pocket - he said he had dropped his tobacco-box; I said I would pick it up for him - he then said he had it in his pocket; I came out of the cook-shop with him, and when I came out I missed a half-crown which I had in my pocket about half an hour before; I charged him with it - he said he had not got it; I had it in my pocket, wrapped up in a piece of paper, when I went into the cook-shop.
Prisoner. Q. Did you make any alarm in the shop? A. No; but I felt your hand in my pocket, and you took hold of my hand immediately - I told you I missed it when I got on the step; there was a hole in my pocket, but the paper could not get through it - the Policeman did not give me a half-crown in the watch-house to try it, and it went through.
EDWARD BARTHOLOMEW. I am a labourer, and live in New-way, Westminster. On the night of Tuesday, the 5th, or rather on Wednesday morning, the 6th of April, I saw the prisoner being taken to the watch-house - I followed him, and saw him put his hand up as if he was going to put it into his waistcoat pocket; I then saw his hand drop, and saw this paper laying twisted up on the ground - I went up to the watch-house, and heard the charge - the prisoner was then set at liberty; I was going back, and the prisoner came to near the same place where I had seen the paper lay - he seemed to be looking about for something, or I should not have thought of looking; I then looked, and found this paper, with half a crown in it - this was in about seven or eight minutes after I first saw it.
Prisoner. I was taken to the watch-house, and this man followed - what money I had about me, which was some copper, was returned to me; I stripped, dressed again, and as I was going away this man stooped down, and said, "I have picked up half a crown;" I was then taken back - the Policeman gave her a half-crown, and it fell through a hole in her pocket - it was a French pocket, in front of her apron; Bartholomew swore he saw me put my hand into my pocket.
EDWARD BARTHOLOMEW. They did take down that you put your hand into your pocket, but I put up my finger, and said, "No, Sir;" I repeated what I had said, that I saw your hand as if you had put your hand into your pocket, but I saw nothing drop - I saw the paper on the ground; I never saw the prosecutrix till that evening; I heard of the charge as I sat in my own house - I went out, and followed him to the watch-house.
Prisoner. I met this girl of the town; she wanted the to go home and sleep with her; I said I had a wife and three children - there was a woman in company with her in the shop, who said, "I know him to be married, so go about your business."
JOHN FREDERICK BOHM. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and found 6 1/2d. in copper, which was given him again; he was then set at liberty, and I took him again.
JURY. Q. Did you try whether a half-crown would go through a hole in her pocket? A. Yes, I tried it; it would go through with squeezing, but not in the paper.
MARY BRUCE. The paper was like that, and I folded it in that way - there was a little mustard on it.
Prisoner's Defence. She is a girl who would swear any thing; I know nothing of her, but giving her a basin of soup - when she found I would not go with her, she gave charge of me.
GUILTY. Aged 35. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-283]
978. JOHN DENNIS was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of March, 2 bottles, value 8s., and 1 glass knob, value 8s., the goods of Thomas Simmons.
ALFRED SMITH. I am shopman to Mr. Thomas Simmons, a glass-man, who lives in Brownlow-street. On the 31st of March I was coming from the back part of the shop, and saw the prisoner in front of the counter - he took up a parcel; I asked him what he wanted, and he told me a gentleman had sent that parcel from Holborn - I said No, it was one I had put on the counter; his breast looked bulky, and I stepped to the door to look for an officer - I turned round, and saw the prisoner placing these bottles and this chandelier knob on the counter, but I did not see from what part of his person he produced them; the constable came in in about half an hour, and I gave the prisoner into custody - these are the bottles and the knob; they are Mr. Simmons' property - there were some odd drops, as patterns in the parcel which he took up; I had received it a short time before, from an out-door workman - these bottles had been on a shelf above the counter, and the knob on a shelf by the side of the counter.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see me put them from my person? A. Yes, I did.
GEORGE JOHN BESTIAUX. I am a Police-constable. The prisoner was placed in my custody.
Prisoner's Defence. I deny ever having them in my possession.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 1 Month.
[trialAccount t18310407-284]
979. ANN BUNKER was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of April, 2 pairs of leather soles, value 2s., and 3 pairs of half-soles, value 2s. 6d., the goods of William Thomas Binckes.
WILLIAM THOMAS BINCKES. I am a leather-seller, and live in Great Newport-street, Soho. On the night of the 5th of April the prisoner came into my shop, a little after eight o'clock, and asked to look at some grained leather, which I put on the counter; I then left the shop, and went into a private passage, from whence I could watch her - I saw her put four pieces of leather into her pocket; I sent my girl for an officer to take her as she went from the shop - he took her; she dropped two pieces of leather, and then another piece; he then searched her, and found three pairs of soles on her - I have known her four or five years; her husband is always laid up sick, and she has three children.
GREGORY COLE DUDLEY. I am a Police-officer. On the night of the 5th of April I took the prisoner about six yards from the prosecutor's door - she dropped two pieces of leather from under her cloak; the prosecutor took them up - I took her farther, and she dropped another piece of sole leather - I then took her into a door-way, and examined her; I found these three pieces of leather wrapped up in her gown.
Prisoner. I took it in a mistake.
GUILTY. Aged 42. - Confined Three Months.

981. JAMES ASHTON was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of March, 1 bonnet, value 12s., the goods of Thomas Cox.
SARAH COX. I am the wife of Thomas Cox, a labourer - we live in Golden-lane. I had a bonnet in a cupboard in one of my rooms on the 21st of March - it was safe about eleven o'clock, and I missed it between eleven and twelve o'clock - this is it; I saw Elizabeth Bulmer with it in her hand last Monday week.
ELIZABETH BULMER. I live in Milton-street, and am the wife of John Bulmer, a bricklayer. On the 21st of March I was in Chapel-street, and saw the prisoner there, between twelve and one o'clock - it is a good step from the prosecutor's; he asked me to buy a bonnet - I said I did not want one; he asked where I lived - I showed him over the way; he came to my house, and brought this bonnet - he asked me 4s. for it; I agreed to give him 3s. - I paid him 1s. 6d. then, and 1s. 6d. the next day; this is the bonnet - I gave it to the officer.
JOHN COHEN. I am Bulmer's brother-in-law. I saw this bargaining about the bonnet when I went home to dinner - the prisoner was sitting in a chair in my room, with it in his hand; and while I was eating my dinner, in came my sister, and bought it of him for 3s.
WILLIAM BALLARD. I am a Police-officer. I received the bonnet from Bulmer - she had it on her head, and the prosecutor claimed it.
JOHN SMITH. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, in Golden-lane, where his father keeps a shop - he mentioned the name of some person who he bought it of, but we could not find him.
Prisoner's Defence. I met a young man I knew that day, and he asked me if I knew any lady who wanted to buy a bonnet - I sold it for him.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18310407-287]
982. MARIA NEEDHAM was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of March, 8 shirts, value 30s.; 2 sheets, value 4s.; 2 table-cloths, value 5s., and 1 handkerchief, value 6d., the goods of James Peckett; and 1 gown, value 2s.; 1 shift, value 1s. 6d., and 1 night-gown, value 1s., the goods of Mary Jenkins.
THOMAS HOBBS KING. I am a Police-officer. On the night of the 29th of March I stopped the prisoner in Maiden-lane, Islington, at half-past nine o'clock - I saw her before I stopped her, in company with a young man, who had this bundle under his arm; he gave it to the prisoner, and they separated - she came on, and he staid in the road; they had not seen me or Harris, who was with me - the prisoner then came on, and as soon as she got sight of me, she dropped the bundle; the young man ran across the fields - this is the bundle, it contains the articles stated; they were wet when I took them.
ALFRED HARRIS. I am a Police-constable. I was with King, and took up the bundle - the prisoner said she knew nothing of it, and that she was one hundred yards from it, but she was not more than five yards from it - she said she knew nothing of the young man; but the next morning she said she was in company with him, and had some conversation with him about some money for some shoes - he goes by the name of Daddy Lee - that was the name she expressed.
JAMES PECKETT. I live in White Conduit-grove, Islington. This bundle contains eight shirts belonging to me, two table-cloths, and the other articles stated - they are all mine, except what are my servants', Mary Jenkins; here is a gown, a shift, and night-gown of hers - they were all hanging in the yard behind my house on the evening of the 29th of March, and were missed at a quarter before nine o'clock - I know nothing of the prisoner.
T. H. KING re-examined. Q. How far from the prosecutor's did you stop the prisoner? A.About three quarters of a mile - it was about half-past nine o'clock.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming down Maiden-lane, and a young man asked me to hold a bundle - directly I took it he ran away.
T. H. KING. No, they were talking together, and she walked ten or fifteen yards before she caught sight of us; there were three more she impeached - the man has absconded ever since.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-288]
983. JOSEPH MOORE was indicted for stealing, onthe 31st of March, 2 pairs of gaiters, value 2s. 6d.; 24 buttons, value 1s., and 1 ball of silk twist, value 2s., the goods of William Gansden.
GEORGE DOSSON. I am a Police-constable. On Thursday evening, the 31st of March, I was in Chiswell-street; I saw the prisoner about twenty minutes after eight o'clock in the evening, with something bulky in his jacket - I followed him a few paces, and asked what he had there; he said Nothing, but I opened his jacket and found the property - I was taking him to the prosecutor, on suspicion, and he said, "If you will let me go this time I will never do the like again."
FRANCES GANSDEN. I am the daughter of William Gansden - he lives in Beech-street, and is a tailor - I am eleven years of age. On Thursday night, the 31st of March, between eight and nine o'clock, I was in my father's shop and saw the prisoner and another boy come in- the other boy asked me the price of some pearl buttons, which were in the window - I sent my sister to ask my father; she came back and said they were 1s. a dozen - the boy looked at them, and said they were flat, not round, and he would tell his mother the price - they then went out, and in ten minutes the officer came - I looked into the window and missed the parcel of pearl buttons - I did not miss the garters - the buttons had been safe till they came in, and the officer brought them back.
WILLIAM GANSDEN. These gaiters are mine; one pair has a ticket on them of my writing - I know I had such buttons as these, and this is the very number that was missed out of the gross - my house is about seven doors from where he was taken.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18310407-289]
984. JOHN JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of March, 1 stove, value 14s., the goods of Stephen Horncastle Geldard.
STEPHEN HORNCASTLE GELDARD. I live in Pullen's-row, Chelsea - I have the care of a house called Ranelagh cottage; it is unoccupied; there are stoves and other fixtures in it. I was there about twelve o'clock in the day on the 5th of March; I fastened the doors when I left; I received some information in about half an hour, and returned; I looked through the parlour window, and saw the prisoner in the parlour, with a stove in his hand; I put the key into the lock, and heard a bolt-shot on the other side - I broke a pane of glass, unfastened the window, and got in; I saw him going out at the back door; he went down the garden - some persons met him, and he jumped into the common sewer; I followed him; he got out, went over a wall, and across a nursery-ground - he was afterwards taken - the stove had been taken out of its place, and set behind the parlour door; I had lost a grate from the same house on the Saturday before - a key was given to me by one of the witnesses, which opened the door very easily.
JAMES CLARK. I am a Police-officer. I received the prisoner about twelve o'clock in the morning of the 5th of March; I found a pad of rags on his shoulder, under his coat.
CHARLES KIDD. I live in Hertford-street. On the 5th of March I was passing the Five-fields, and saw the prisoner running; I stopped him, and said, "My friend, where are you running? what is all this hue and cry?" he said, "It is only a parcel of boys hunting me" - I said, "It is very curions, wait till they come up;" he said,"Oh! no, let me go" - I said No! and stopped him; he threw out his right hand, and threw away a key - I pointed out the place where it was thrown.
EDWARD ROSER. I live in Ebury-square, Pimlico - I am a carrier. On the 5th of March I was in Union-street, I saw the prisoner come running down the street, and some persons were calling Stop theif! I saw the prisoner stopped by a person a few yards from me; I saw him throw something away - I took it up, and it was this key.
MR. GELDARD. This key will open the room door. and I found this piece of iron in the passage just by the back door - I had lost eight stoves before.
JAMES CLARK. I saw the prisoner go into the sewer, up to his knees.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going down the lane, and was taken had in my inside; I got over the rails, and heard a cry of Stop thief! I crossed the ditch, and went into the privy; I then got over a wall into the road, and a person stopped me, but I can take my solemn oath I threw nothing out of my hand - I never was in the house.
GUILTY. Aged 23. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18310407-290]
985. JAMES HEATH and JOHN BAILES were indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of February, 3 pairs of shoes, value 3s., and 1 brush, value 6d., the goods of John Dickenson.
BENJAMIN FOSTER. I live in Paradise-street, St. Luke's, and am a labourer. On the 22nd of February I was coming up Paradise-place, about three o'clock; I saw three lads; the prisoners were two of them; they were standing altogether - Heath had some shoes in the bosom of his coat; I saw the heels of them sticking out; I went to the prosecutor's shop, which is in Tabernacle-walk, close by, and spoke to his wife; she said there were three pairs of shoes gone - Mr. Dickenson came up, and he got me to go with him in pursuit of the boys, who had all run away; I came up to the prisoners in Pitfield-street - the prosecutor and the Police-officer had then secured them.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You did not see Heath do any thing? A. No - the boy who is not in custody was the oldest; Heath did not say the other gave them to him to hold - he seemed frightened; we lost sight of the other all at once.
ROBERT RODMAN. I was servant to Mr. Woodward, a cabinet-maker, who lives in Old-street-road. On the 22nd of February I was standing at his door, and saw the two prisoners run by - Heath threw three pairs of shoes and a bottle-brush at me; they were running by as fast as they could - I took the shoes and brush to the back of the shop, placed them under one of the tables, and the Police-officer came and had them.
Cross-examined. Q.Did he not tell you he was tired of them, and that another gentleman gave them to him? A. No, he did not say where he got them.
GEORGE DICKENSON. I am a broker - I live in Tabernacle-walk. On the 22nd of February , in consequence of information from Foster and my wife, I went in pursuit of some boys to Old-street-road - I got sight of the two prisoners and another , going towards Shoreditch ; they turned down a street , then across Hoxton-market, and out by Hoxton new church - I went on to Pitfield-street, came up with the prisoners in Royal Oak-walk, and took them; these are my shoes and brush - I had not been out of the shop a quarter of an hour .
Cross-examined. Q. I suppose you have some mark on them? A. I can swear to the shoes, by this break in the grain of one - they hung on the door; a person standing on the steps might take them without going into the shop.
JOHN OWEN. I am a Police-constable. I saw the prisoners in Pitfield-street, and assisted in taking them at the top of the court; I received the shoes and brush from Rodman, tied together as they are now .
Bailes' Defence. We were going along, and saw some boys with the shoes and brush - we heard a cry of Stop thief! they ran and we ran .
Heath's Defence. I never saw the shoes; I was going for some potatoes .
The prisoners received a good character.
HEATH - GUILTY. Aged 13.
BAILES - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor and Jury.
Whipped and Discharged .
[trialAccount t18310407-291]
986. JOHN DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of March, 1 African dress robe, value 3l., the goods of Maria Lardner.
MARIA LARDNER. I live in Acre-cottage, Camden-town, and am a widow. The prisoner was in my son's service; he is an officer in the Second West India Regiment - in September last my son and the prisoner left my house to go abroad, but in a few days the ship got leaky, and returned to Margate; my son went to an hotel, the prisoner left him, and came back to my house - he continued there till the 24th of March, when I sent him away , and when he was gone I missed the robe of an African chief, which my son had brought from abroad, and made me a present of - I went to the house of Martha Baggs, and got the robe from her.
GEORGE BROWN. I am a Police-constable. I took the prisoner in Pratt-street, on the 24th of March; I told him I took him on suspicion of stealing this dress - he said his mistress gave it him to put on his bed, to keep him warm ; Martha Baggs is not here .
COURT to MRS. LARDNER. Q. Do you know whether it was used on the prisoner's bed? A. He complained of being cold (as they are always very chilly), and wished to have more clothes - I think he had this on his bed; he has been always robbing me.
NOT GUILTY.
